Heavy Mesons to Charmed Tetraquark Decays
Chun-Khiang Chua

Abstract
Motivated by the recent observations of T c ​ s ​ 0 ∗ ​ ( 2870 ) 0 T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0} , T c ​ s ¯ ​ 0 ∗ ​ ( 2900 ) 0 T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0} and T c ​ s ¯ ​ 0 ∗ ​ ( 2900 ) + ⁣ + T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++} charmed tetraquark states by LHCb, we study the decays of heavy mesons to these charmed tetraquark states ( T T ) using a topological amplitude approach. We first obtain the T → D ​ P T\to DP and D ​ S DS strong decay amplitudes by decomposing them into several topological amplitudes, where P P is a light pseudo-scalar particle and S S is a low-lying scalar particle. Later on, weak decay amplitudes of B ¯ → D ​ T ¯ \overline{B}\to D\overline{T} , D ¯ ​ T \overline{D}T and B ¯ → T ​ P \overline{B}\to TP , T ​ S TS decays are also decomposed topologically. In addition, B c − → T ​ T ¯ B^{-}_{c}\to T\overline{T} decays are also discussed. Using these results, modes with an unambiguous exotic interpretation in flavor are highlighted.



I
Introduction

In 2020, LHCb reported the observation of a charmed tetraquark
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}

in
B^{-}\to D^{-}D^{+}K^{-}
decay with
B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0},T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}\to D^{+}K^{-}
decay
LHCb:2020pxc
.
From
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}\to D^{+}K^{-}
decay, the minimal quark content of
cs\bar{u}\bar{d}
for
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
is infered.
Several years later, the observations of two other charmed tetraquarks,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
, are reported in

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}D_{s}^{-}\pi^{+}
and
B^{-}\to D^{+}D_{s}^{-}\pi^{-}
decays with

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{0},\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{0}\to D_{s}^{-}\pi^{+}

and

B^{-}\to D^{+}\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{\,--},\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{\,--}\to D_{s}^{-}\pi^{-}
decays

LHCb:2022sfr
;
LHCb:2022lzp
.
From
{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\pi^{-}
and
{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{\,++}\to D_{s}^{+}\pi^{+}
decays the minimal quark contents of these states are
c\bar{s}\bar{u}d
and
c\bar{s}u\bar{d}
, respectively.
The masses and widths of these states are
ParticleDataGroup:2024cfk

\displaystyle m(T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2.872\pm 0.016{\rm GeV},\quad\Gamma(T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0})=0.067\pm 0.024{\rm GeV},

\displaystyle m(T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2.892\pm 0.021{\rm GeV},\quad\Gamma(T^{*}_{cs0}(2900)^{0})=0.119\pm 0.029{\rm GeV},

\displaystyle m(T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2.921\pm 0.026{\rm GeV},\quad\Gamma(T^{*}_{cs0}(2900)^{++})=0.140\pm 0.040{\rm GeV}.

(1)

Very recently, LHCb also reports the observation of a new decay mode of
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
, namely
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}\to D^{0}K^{0}_{S}
,
in
B^{-}\to D^{-}D^{0}K^{0}_{S}
decay,
and the relative rates of
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{K}^{0}
and
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}\to D^{+}K^{-}
decays is consistent with isospin symmetry
within the experimental uncertainty
LHCb:2024xyx
.

There are many theoretical interpretations of these states; see, for example, refs.

Karliner:2020vsi
;
He:2020jna
;
Wang:2020xyc
;
Molina:2020hde
;
Huang:2020ptc
;
Liu:2020nil
;
Wang:2021lwy
;
Yue:2022mnf
;
Burns:2020epm
;
Liu:2020orv

see also ref.
PDGreview
.
In this work, we will study the decays of heavy mesons to these charmed tetraquark states (
T
).
As a direct calculation of the decay amplitudes is too complicated, we will make use of a topological amplitude approach,
which have been applied to many heavy meson decays, see, for example,
refs.
Zeppenfeld:1980ex
;
Chau:tk
;
Chau:1990ay
;
Gronau:1994rj
;
Gronau:1995hn
;
Chiang:2004nm
;
Cheng:2014rfa
;
Savage:ub
;
Li:2012cfa
;
Qin:2013tje
;
Chua:2003it
;
Chua:2013zga
;
Cheng:2015cca
;
Chua:2016aqy
;
Chua:2022wmr
;
Chua:2023qyj
.
In particular, in ref.
Cheng:2015cca
, we applied this method to heavy baryons and pentaquark decays, obtaining results later confirmed experimentally
LHCb:2016lve
.
Note that a study on tetraquark production in
B
decays using diagrammatic analysis was given in ref.
Qin:2022nof
.
We will compare our results with those obtained in that work later.

In this work, we will consider two scenarios where the light antiquarks in the tetraquarks are either antisymmetric or symmetric.
It will be interesting to see which scenario the above tetraquarks, namely
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
, fit.
We will consider the strong decays of tetraquarks and weak decays involving tetraquarks.
Attentions are paid to predictions where all
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
are involved.
We will try to identify other flavor exotic states.
It will be useful to identify modes involving flavor exotic states.
We will be in a better position to understand these tetraquarks with this information.

The layout of this paper is as follows.
We introduce two different scenarios of the light quark pair
\bar{q}\bar{q}
in the charmed tetraquarks
T_{cq\bar{q}\bar{q}}
in section II.
All flavor exotic states are identified.
We consider

T\to DP
and
DS
decays in Sec. III,

\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
and
\overline{D}T
decays in Sec. IV,

\overline{B}\to TP
and
TS
decays in Sec. V
and
B_{c}\to T\overline{T}
decays in Sec. VI.
Decay amplitudes of these decays are given in terms of topological amplitudes.
These results can be easily generalized to modes with
D^{*}
and
V
, instead of
D
and
P
, respectively.
Modes with unambiguous exotic interpretations in flavor are highlighted.
Sec. VII comes to the conclusions.
We end this paper with two appendices.

II
Two different configurations of the light anti-quarks in
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}

II.1
Scenarios I and II

The tetraquark
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}
has two antiquarks.
We can decompose the anti-quark pair
\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}
into anti-symmetric
[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}]
and symmetric
\{\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}\}
at the outset,
since SU(3) transformation does not mix up these two sets.
Hence, in this work, we consider two different configurations for the light anti-quarks in a
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}
state, namely an antisymmetric
\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}
configuration and a symmetric
\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}
configuration.
They can be further separated into traceless and traceful parts, which do not mix under SU(3) transformation, see Appendix
A
.

For the antisymmetric
\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}
configuration, the nine
q[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}]
are SU(3)
{\bf 3}\otimes{\bf 3}
states, which can be expressed in a
3\times 3
matrix given by

\displaystyle{\bf T}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}T_{cu[\bar{d}\bar{s}]}^{++}&T_{cu[\bar{s}\bar{u}]}^{+}&T_{cu[\bar{u}\bar{d}]}^{+}\\
T_{cd[\bar{d}\bar{s}]}^{+}&T_{cd[\bar{s}\bar{u}]}^{0}&T_{cd[\bar{u}\bar{d}]}^{0}\\
T_{cs[\bar{d}\bar{s}]}^{+}&T_{cs[\bar{s}\bar{u}]}^{0}&T_{cs[\bar{u}\bar{d}]}^{0}\end{array}\right),

where the
[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}]
notation indicates the antisymmetric configuration of these light anti-quarks.
These nine
{\bf 3}\times{\bf 3}
states can be grouped into a
{\bf 6}
and a
\bar{\bf 3}
, see Eq. (
137
),
where the
{\bf 6}
is the traceless part,
and it consists of an
S=+1
isotriplet,
(T^{++}_{c\bar{s}},T^{+}_{c\bar{s}},T^{0}_{c\bar{s}})
, an
S=-1
isosinglet,
T^{0}_{cs}
,
and an
S=0
isodoublet,
(T_{c}^{+},T_{c}^{0})
,
while the
\bar{\bf 3}
is the traceful part,
and it has an
S=0
isodoublet,
(T^{\prime\prime+}_{c},T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c})
, and an
S=+1
isosinglet,
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}
, see Fig.
1
(a).
The quark contents and quantum numbers of these states are given in Table
1
.
Consequently, the above
3\times 3
matrix
{\bf T}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle{\bf T}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}T_{c\bar{s}}^{++}&-\frac{T_{c\bar{s}}^{+}+T_{c\bar{s}}^{\prime\prime+}}{\sqrt{2}}&\frac{T_{c}^{+}+T_{c}^{\prime\prime+}}{\sqrt{2}}\\
-\frac{T_{c\bar{s}}^{+}-T_{c\bar{s}}^{\prime\prime+}}{\sqrt{2}}&-T_{c\bar{s}}^{0}&\frac{T_{c}^{0}-T_{c}^{\prime\prime 0}}{\sqrt{2}}\\
\frac{T_{c}^{+}-T_{c}^{\prime\prime+}}{\sqrt{2}}&\frac{T_{c}^{0}+T_{c}^{\prime\prime 0}}{\sqrt{2}}&T_{cs}^{0}\end{array}\right).

Note that for convenience we number these
T
in Table
1
and add asterisks to flavor exotic states.

In this scenario, namely scenario I,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
,
T_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
are in the
{\bf 6}
.
In particular,

T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
and
T_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
are
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}
and
T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}
, respectively, belonging to the
S=1
isotriplet,
while
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
is
T^{0}_{cs}
in the
S=-1
isosinglet as shown in Table
1
.
The three flavor exotic states are labeled by asterisks in the table.

Mixing can happen for particles in different SU(3) multiplets, but mixing for particles in different isospin multiplets is expected to be more difficult.
Consequently,
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
in the
S=1
isotripet does not mix with
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}
or
D_{s}
.
Therefore, it should also be considered a flavor exotic particle and be labeled with an asterisk in the table.
To our knowledge, such a particle has not been considered a flavor exotic particle in the literature.

To match the flavor of these states, we can define the following tensor field,

\displaystyle T^{i}_{[jk]}\equiv\epsilon_{jkl}{\bf T}^{il}.

(10)

Note that from the above equations, we have

\displaystyle T^{i}_{[i1]}=-T^{i}_{[1i]}=\sqrt{2}T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c},\quad T^{i}_{[i2]}=-T^{i}_{[2i]}=\sqrt{2}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c},\quad T^{i}_{[i3]}=-T^{i}_{[3i]}=\sqrt{2}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}},

(11)

where only the
\bar{\bf 3}
states remain after taking the trace
T^{i}_{[ik]}
, as the
{\bf 6}
is traceless, where one can also infer from Table
1
.

\bar{q}\bar{q}^{\prime}
.

{\bf 6}
and a
\bar{\bf 3}
.
We numbered these states and states with flavor exotic are labeled by asterisks.
In this scenario,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
,

and

are in the

.

We now turn to the second scenario, scenario II, where the light anti-quarks are symmetric.
For the
q\{\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}\}
configuration, we have eighteen SU(3)
{\bf 3}\otimes\bar{\bf 6}
states.
They form a
\overline{\bf 15}
and another
\bar{\bf 3}
, see Eq. (
137
).
The
\overline{\bf 15}
is the traceless part, which consists of six iso-multiplets:
an
S=+1
isotriplet,
(T^{++}_{c\bar{s}},T^{+}_{c\bar{s}},T^{0}_{c\bar{s}})
,
an
S=-1
isotriplet,
(T^{+}_{cs},T^{0}_{cs},T^{-}_{cs})
,
an
S=+2
isodoublet,
(T^{++}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}},T^{+}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}})
,
an
S=0
iso-quarplet,
(T_{c}^{++},T_{c}^{+},T_{c}^{0},T_{c}^{-})
,
an
S=0
isodoublet
(T_{c}^{\prime+},T_{c}^{\prime 0})

and an
S=+1
isosinglet,
T_{c\bar{s}}^{\prime+}
,
while the
\bar{\bf 3}
is the traceful part, and it has an
S=0
isodoublet,
(T^{\prime\prime+}_{c},T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c})
, and an
S=+1
isosinglet,
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}
, see Fig.
1
(b).
The quark contents and quantum numbers of these states are given in Table
2
,
and as in scenario I, we label these
T
in Table
2
and add asterisks to flavor exotic states.

Note that in this scenario,

T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
,
T_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
are in the
\overline{\bf 15}
.
In particular,

T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
and
T_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
are
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}
and
T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}
, respectively, belonging to the
S=1
isotriplet,
while
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
is
T^{0}_{cs}
belonging to another isotriplet with
S=-1
.
In this scenario, however, there are six other flavor exotic states similar to the above states, namely,

T^{+}_{cs}
.

T^{-}_{cs}
,

T^{++}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}
,

T^{++}_{c}
,
and

T^{-}_{c}
.
In fact,
T^{+}_{cs}
,
T^{-}_{cs}
and
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
belong to the same isotriplet (with
S=-1
),
while
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}
,

T^{++}_{c}

and

T^{-}_{c}

belong to the
S=-2
isodouble and the iso-quarplet with
S=0
, respectively, as shown in Table
2
. These nine flavor exotic states are labeled with asterisks in the table. They have been identified in the literature, see, for example, ref.
Qin:2022nof
, where a different notation was used.

Furthermore, as mentioned previously, mixing for particles in different isospin multiplets is expected to be more difficult to occur.
Consequently,
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
in the
S=+1
isotripet,

T^{+}_{c}
and
T^{0}_{c}
in the
S=0
iso-quarplet
do not mix with
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}
,
D_{s}
,
T^{\prime+}_{c}
,
T^{\prime 0}_{c}
or
D^{+,0}
.
They should also be considered flavor exotic particles and be labeled with asterisks in the table.
To our knowledge, these particles have not been considered flavor exotic particles in the literature.
Altogether, we have twelve flavor exotic states in this scenario.

The tensor fields corresponding to states in scenario II are defined as follows,

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\{23\}}=T^{++}_{cu\{\bar{d}\bar{s}\}}=T^{++}_{cs},

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\{31\}}=T^{+}_{cu\{\bar{s}\bar{u}\}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}},

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\{22\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{++}_{cu\{\bar{d}\bar{d}\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{++}_{c},

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\{11\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{0}_{cu\{\bar{u}\bar{u}\}}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}T^{0}_{c}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}T^{\prime 0}_{c}+T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c},

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\{33\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{++}_{cu\{\bar{s}\bar{s}\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{++}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}},

\displaystyle T^{1}_{\{12\}}=T^{+}_{cu\{\bar{u}\bar{d}\}}=\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}T^{+}_{c}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}T^{\prime+}_{c}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c},

(12)

\displaystyle T^{2}_{\{23\}}=T^{+}_{cd\{\bar{d}\bar{s}\}}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}},

\displaystyle T^{2}_{\{31\}}=T^{0}_{cd\{\bar{s}\bar{u}\}}=T^{0}_{c\bar{s}},

\displaystyle T^{2}_{\{12\}}=T^{0}_{cd\{\bar{u}\bar{d}\}}=-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}T^{0}_{c}+\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}T^{\prime 0}_{c}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c},

\displaystyle T^{2}_{\{11\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{-}_{cd\{\bar{u}\bar{u}\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{-}_{c},

\displaystyle T^{2}_{\{22\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{+}_{cd\{\bar{d}\bar{d}\}}=-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}T^{+}_{c}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}T^{\prime+}_{c}+T^{\prime\prime+}_{c},

\displaystyle T^{2}_{\{33\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{+}_{cd\{\bar{s}\bar{s}\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}},

(13)

and

\displaystyle T^{3}_{\{23\}}=T^{+}_{cs\{\bar{d}\bar{s}\}}=-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}T^{\prime+}_{c}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c},

\displaystyle T^{3}_{\{31\}}=T^{0}_{cs\{\bar{s}\bar{u}\}}=-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}T^{\prime 0}_{c}+\frac{1}{2}T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c},

\displaystyle T^{3}_{\{12\}}=T^{0}_{cs\{\bar{u}\bar{d}\}}=T^{0}_{cs},

\displaystyle T^{3}_{\{11\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{-}_{cs\{\bar{u}\bar{u}\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{-}_{cs},

\displaystyle T^{3}_{\{22\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{+}_{cs\{\bar{d}\bar{d}\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{+}_{cs},

\displaystyle T^{3}_{\{33\}}=\sqrt{2}T^{+}_{cs\{\bar{s}\bar{s}\}}=-T^{\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}+T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}},

(14)

where we use
\{\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime}\}
to indicates the symmetric configuration of these light anti-quarks.
Note that from the above equations, we have

\displaystyle T^{i}_{\{i1\}}=T^{i}_{\{1i\}}=2T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c},\quad T^{i}_{\{i2\}}=T^{i}_{\{2i\}}=2T^{\prime\prime+}_{c},\quad T^{i}_{\{i3\}}=T^{i}_{\{3i\}}=2T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}},

(15)

where only the
\bar{\bf 3}^{\prime}
states remain after taking the trace,
T^{i}_{\{ik\}}
, as the
\overline{\bf 15}
is traceless, where one can also see from Table
2
.

II.2
Remarks

In this work, we consider
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
either in the
{\bf 6}
as in scenario I or in the
\overline{\bf 15}
as in scenario II.
In principle, they may belong to different multiplets.
For example, we may have
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
belonging to the
{\bf 6}
, while
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
belonging to the
\overline{\bf 15}
, or vice verse.
It is our working assumption that they only belong to one of the multiplets and work out the consequences.
It is then useful to verify those predictions experimentally to check the working assumption.
Therefore, we should pay attention to predictions where all
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
are involved, or, at least,

T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
and either one of
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
are involved.

Before we end this section, it should be pointed out that, in principle, particles in scenarios I and II can mix.
It is our working assumption that they do not.
Even if they do, the amplitudes obtained in this work are still useful, since amplitudes for mixed states can be built upon them.
Nevertheless, not all flavor exotic states can mix.
Although
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}
,
T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}
and
T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}
in scenarios I and II can,

T_{cs}^{0}
s cannot, as they have different isospin quantum numbers.
In addition, the rest of the flavor exotic states in scenario II cannot, as they do not have any counterparts in scenario I, see Fig.
1
.
Furthermore, all flavor exotic states identified in this section do not mix with
D
or any other
c\bar{q}
states in any case.
They are exotic.

III
T\to DP
and
T\to DS
decays

III.1
T\to DP
decays

We consider the strong decays of
T\to DP
decays in this sub-section.
We will first discuss how to decompose these decay amplitudes into topological amplitudes.
Then
T\to DP
decay amplitudes will be decomposed accordingly.

III.1.1
Topological amplitudes in
T\to DP
decays

In scenario I the Hamiltonian governing
T(cq[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}])\to DP
decays is given by

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{DP}\,T^{i}_{[jk]}\overline{D}^{j}\Pi^{k}_{i}+AC_{P}\,T^{i}_{[ik]}\overline{D}^{r}\Pi^{k}_{r}+AC_{D}\,T^{i}_{[ik]}\overline{D}^{k}\Pi^{r}_{r},

(16)

while in scenario II the Hamiltonian for
T(cq\{\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}\})\to DP
decays is given by

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{DP}\,T^{i}_{\{jk\}}\overline{D}^{j}\Pi^{k}_{i}+AC_{P}\,T^{i}_{\{ik\}}\overline{D}^{r}\Pi^{k}_{r}+AC_{D}\,T^{i}_{\{ik\}}\overline{D}^{k}\Pi^{r}_{r},

(17)

with

\displaystyle D=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}D^{0}&D^{+}&D^{+}_{s}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\Pi=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{\pi^{0}}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\cos\theta\,\eta+\sin\theta\,\eta^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}&\pi^{+}&K^{+}\\
\pi^{-}&-\frac{\pi^{0}}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\cos\theta\,\eta+\sin\theta\,\eta^{\prime}}{\sqrt{2}}&K^{0}\\
K^{-}&\overline{K}^{0}&\cos\theta\,\eta^{\prime}-\sin\theta\,\eta\end{array}\right),

and

\displaystyle\theta=(39.3\pm 1.0)^{\circ},

(24)

the
\eta
–
\eta^{\prime}
mixing angle
Feldmann:1998sh
.
Note that although we use the same notations,
namely
F_{DP}
,
AC_{P}
and
AC_{D}
, in Eqs. (
16
) and (
17
) for simplicity of notation, they are, in principle, different.

Figure 2:
Topological diagrams of
(a)
F_{DP}
(fall-apart), (b)
AC_{P}
(annihilation-creation) and (c)
AC_{D}
(annihilation-creation)
amplitudes in
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime}}\to DP
decays.
The subscripts indicate the final state meson(s) receiving the light quark and/or the anti-quarks of
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}
.
These are flavor flow diagrams.
The decay amplitudes of tetraquarks in the multiplet containing flavor exotic states can only have contributions from diagram (a).

As shown in the above effective Hamiltonian, there are three topological amplitudes in
T\to DP
decays,
where

F_{DP}
is a fall-apart amplitude,

AC_{P}
and
AC_{D}
are annihilation and creation amplitudes.
The topological amplitudes are depicted in Fig.
2
.
The subscripts indicate the final state meson(s) receiving the light quark and/or the anti-quarks of
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}
.
For example, for
AC_{P}
, the antiquark of
T
goes into
P
, see the diagram in Fig.
2
(b).
It is reasonable to expect that the fall-apart amplitude is dominating, i.e.

\displaystyle|F_{DP}|\gg|AC_{P}|,|AC_{D}|.

(25)

Furthermore, the decay amplitudes of tetraquarks in the
{\bf 6}
in scenario I and in the
\overline{\bf 15}
in scenario II can only have contributions from diagram (a), the fall-apart amplitude,
F_{DP}
. This can be easily seen, as the
AC_{P}
and
AC_{D}
terms in Eqs. (
16
) and (
17
) necessary involve the trace
T^{i}_{[ik]}
or
T^{i}_{\{ik\}}
, where only the states in the
\bar{\bf 3}
in scenario I and the
\bar{\bf 3}^{\prime}
in scenario II remain, see Eqs.(
11
) and (
15
).
In fact, the reason for the above facts can be easily understood by noting
\bar{\bf 3}\otimes{\bf 8}=\overline{\bf 15}\oplus{\bf 6}\oplus\bar{\bf 3}
, see Eq. (
138
).
Hence there is only one
\overline{\bf 15}
and one
{\bf 6}
in
D\Pi
final states, which can match the
\overline{\bf 15}
of
T
in scenario II and
{\bf 6}
of
T
in scenario I, respectively. Adding
\eta_{1}
in
P
does not affect the above matching.

With the above effective Hamiltonians, we can decompose
T\to DP
decay amplitudes in terms of these topological amplitudes.
The
T\to DP
decay amplitudes for
T=T(cq[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}])
in scenario I and
T=T(cq\{\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}\})
in scenario II are shown in Table
3
and
4
, respectively.
Some of the modes may be kinematically prohibited, but the information on these amplitudes can still be useful, as the tetraquark can be a virtual particle in some other processes.

From Table
3
and
4
, we see that for flavor exotic states and states in the same multiplets, namely
{\bf 6}
in scenario I and
\overline{\bf 15}
in scenario II, the
T\to DP
decay amplitudes only consist of the fall-apart amplitude, i.e.
F_{DP}
.
Their decay amplitudes are all highly related.

In fact, for flavor exotic states in scenario I, most of their decay amplitudes are identical up to a sign.
As one can infer from Table
3
, the squares of the amplitudes of the following six modes,
namely

T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{+}K^{+},D_{s}^{+}\pi^{+}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}\pi^{0}
,

T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{0}K^{0},D_{s}^{+}\pi^{-}

and

T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{0}\overline{K}^{0},D^{+}K^{-}
decays,
should be identical,
while those in
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{0}K^{+}
and
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{+}K^{0}
decays are half the above.
The flavor exotic nature of most of these states is self-evident from the table.
Note that in
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}\pi^{0}
decay the exotic quantum number of
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
can also be easily identified.

The three other states in
{\bf 6}
, namely,
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
,
T^{+}_{c}
and
T^{0}_{c}
, have
T\to DP
decay rates that are proportional to the previous ones with simple factors.
The situation is different for the three states in
\bar{\bf 3}
, namely
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c}
,
T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c}
and
T^{\prime\prime+}_{cs}
, as their amplitudes contain
F_{DP}
,
AC_{P}
and
AC_{D}
as well.
Nevertheless, the
T\to DP
decay amplitudes of these states for
P=\pi
,
K
and
\overline{K}
, are proportional to
F_{DP}+2AC_{P}
, giving

\Gamma(T\to D\pi^{+})=\Gamma(T\to D\overline{K})=\Gamma(T\to DK)=2\Gamma(T\to D\pi^{0})
for these states.
As noted previously we have
\bar{\bf 3}\otimes{\bf 8}=\overline{\bf 15}\oplus{\bf 6}\oplus\bar{\bf 3}
, see Eq. (
138
).
The
\bar{\bf 3}
in the
D\Pi
final states can match the
\bar{\bf 3}
of
T
in scenario I, but the addition of
\eta_{1}
in
P
provides another
\bar{\bf 3}
in
DP
and another combination,
namely
F_{DP}-3AC_{D}-AC_{P}
,
is needed for the
T\to D\eta,D\eta^{\prime}
decay amplitudes.

Similarly, for scenario II, as one can infer from Table
4
, the following modes,
namely,

T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{+}K^{+},D_{s}^{+}\pi^{+}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}\pi^{0}
,

T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{0}K^{0},D_{s}^{+}\pi^{-}

and

T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{0}\overline{K}^{0},D^{+}K^{-}
decays,
should have identical amplitudes squared.
The amplitudes squared of

T^{+}_{cs}\to D^{+}\overline{K}^{0}
,

T^{-}_{cs}\to D^{0}K^{-}
,

T^{++}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}K^{+}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}K^{0}
,

T^{++}_{c}\to D^{+}\pi^{+}

and

T^{-}_{c}\to D^{0}\pi^{-}
decays are doubled compared to the former modes.
In addition, for the other two flavor exotic states, we have
T^{+}_{c}\to D^{0}\pi^{+},D^{+}\pi^{0}
,
T^{0}_{c}\to D^{0}\pi^{0}
and
D^{+}\pi^{-}
decays with amplitudes squared porpotianal to the aboves.
The flavor exotic nature of most of these states is self-evident from the table.
For
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
, the
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}\pi^{0}
decay reveals the exotic nature of
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
,
but for
T^{+}_{c}
and
T^{0}_{c}
, elebrated works are required,
as one needs to verify that they do not decay to
D\eta
and
D\eta^{\prime}
.

Furthermore, four other states in
\overline{\bf 15}
, namely,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
,

T^{\prime+}_{c}
,

T^{\prime 0}_{c}

and

T^{\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}
,
have
T\to DP
decay amplitudes squared proportional to the previous ones with some simple factors,
while the situation is slightly different for the three states in
\bar{\bf 3}^{\prime}
,
namely
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c}
,
T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c}
and
T^{\prime\prime+}_{cs}
, as their amplitudes contain
F_{DP}
,
AC_{P}
and
AC_{D}
as well.
Nevertheless, as shown in Table
4
, the
T\to DP
decay amplitudes of these states for
P=\pi
,
K
and
\overline{K}
, are proportional to the combination of
F_{DP}+4AC_{P}
, giving
\Gamma(T\to D\pi^{+})=\Gamma(T\to D\overline{K})=\Gamma(T\to DK)=2\Gamma(T\to D\pi^{0})
for these states.
The
\bar{\bf 3}
in the
D\Pi
final states can match the
\bar{\bf 3}^{\prime}
of
T
in scenario II, but the addition of
\eta_{1}
in
P
provide another
\bar{\bf 3}
in
DP
,
where another combination, namely
3AC_{D}+\sqrt{2}(F_{DP}+AC_{P})
, is needed for the
T\to D\eta,D\eta^{\prime}
decay amplitudes.

Recently, LHCb found that at the current experimental precision, the relative rates of

T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)\to D^{+}K^{-}
and
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)\to D^{0}\overline{K}{}^{0}
decays are consistent with an isospin invariant
LHCb:2024xyx
.
This result supports the above results, where in the

T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{0}\overline{K}^{0},D^{+}K^{-}
modes we have

|A(T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{0}\overline{K}^{0})|=|A(T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{+}K^{-})|
in both scenarios.

III.2
T\to DS
decays

III.2.1
Topological amplitudes in
T\to DS
decays

The Hamiltonian in scenario I governing
T(cq[\bar{q}\bar{q}^{\prime}])\to DS
decays is given by

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{S}\,T^{i}_{[jk]}\overline{D}^{p}S^{kj}_{pi}+C_{DS}\,T^{i}_{[jk]}\overline{D}^{j}S^{kp}_{pi}+ACC_{S}\,T^{i}_{[ik]}\overline{D}^{r}S^{kp}_{pr}+ACC_{D}\,T^{i}_{[ik]}\overline{D}^{k}S^{rp}_{pr},

(26)

while the Hamiltonian in scenario II for
T(cq\{\bar{q}\bar{q}^{\prime}\})\to DS
decays is given by,

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{DS}\,T^{i}_{\{jk\}}\overline{D}^{j}S^{kp}_{pi}+ACC_{S}\,T^{i}_{\{ik\}}\overline{D}^{r}S^{kp}_{pr}+ACC_{D}\,T^{i}_{\{ik\}}\overline{D}^{k}S^{rp}_{pr}.

(27)

Note that in the above equations,
S^{ij}_{kl}
denotes the field for low-lying scalar particles with

\displaystyle S^{ij}_{kl}=\epsilon^{ijp}{\bf S}_{p}^{q}\epsilon_{qkl}=S([q^{i}q^{j}][\bar{q}_{k}\bar{q}_{l}]),

(28)

with

\displaystyle{\bf S}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{a^{0}_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\cos\phi\,f_{0}-\sin\phi\,\sigma}{\sqrt{2}}&a^{+}_{0}&\kappa^{+}\\
a^{-}_{0}&-\frac{a^{0}_{0}}{\sqrt{2}}+\frac{\cos\phi\,f_{0}-\sin\phi\,\sigma}{\sqrt{2}}&\kappa^{0}\\
\kappa^{-}&\bar{\kappa}^{0}&\sin\phi\,f_{0}+\cos\phi\,\sigma\end{array}\right),

the mixing angle is taken to be
\phi=174.6^{\circ}
Maiani:2004uc
.
For simplicity of notation, we do not distinguish the topological amplitudes in scenario I and scenario II, which, however, do not imply that they are identical.
We follow refs.
Jaffe:1976ig
;
Jaffe:1976ih
on the quark contents of these low-lying scalar particles.
For more information on the flavor structure of these particles, see Appendix
B
.

Figure 3:
Topological diagrams of
(a)
C_{S}
(creation) (only for
T_{cq[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}]}
decays), (b)
C_{DS}
(creation), (c)
ACC_{S}
(annihilation-double creation)
and (d)
ACC_{D}
(annihilation-double creation)
amplitudes in
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime}}\to DS
decays.
The subscripts indicate the final state meson(s) receiving the light quark and/or the anti-quarks of
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}
.
The decays of flavor exotic states can only have diagrams (a) and (b) in scenario I,
but for scenario II only diagram (b) is allowed for flavor exotic states.

There are four topological amplitudes for
T\to DS
decays in scenario I (
T=T(cq[\bar{q}\bar{q}^{\prime}])
),
where
C_{S}
,
C_{DS}
are creation amplitudes with a
q\bar{q}
pair created in the decay processes, see Fig.
3
(a) and (b),
and
ACC_{S}
and
ACC_{D}
are annihilation-double-creation amplitudes with a
q\bar{q}
pair annihilated and two
q\bar{q}
pairs created, see Fig.
3
(c) and (d).
The subscripts of these topological amplitudes denote the final state mesons to which the quarks and antiquark from
T
go.
In scenario II (
T=T(cq\{\bar{q}\bar{q}^{\prime}\})
),
it is evident that a
T(cq\{\bar{q}\bar{q}^{\prime}\})\to DS
decay cannot have a
C_{S}
amplitude, as their flavors cannot match,
since the anti-quark pair in
T(cq\{\bar{q}\bar{q}^{\prime}\})
is symmetric, while those in
S
is antisymmetric.

As shown in Fig.
3
,
we do not have fall-apart amplitude
F
in this
T\to DS
decays.
However, we expect creation amplitudes
C_{S,DS}
to dominate over annihilation-double creation amplitudes
ACC_{S,D}
in
these decays, as the former involves only
q\bar{q}
creation, while the latter needs a
q\bar{q}
pair annihilated and two
q\bar{q}
pairs created.

Furthermore, diagrams (c) and (d) in Fig.
3
involve the traces
T^{i}_{[ik]}
and
T^{i}_{\{ik\}}
,
where only
\bar{\bf 3}
and
\bar{\bf 3}^{\prime}
remain.
Therefore, in scenario I the decays of flavor exotic states and all other states in
{\bf 6}
can only have diagrams (a) and (b),
while for scenario II only diagram (b) is allowed for flavor exotic states and all other states in
\overline{\bf 15}
.

The
T_{cq\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}}\to DS
decay amplitudes decomposed in these topological amplitudes are shown in Table
5
and
6
for scenarios I and II, respectively.
where we have
T=T(cq[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}])
and
T(cq\{\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}\})
, respectively.
Note that although some of the decay modes listed in these tables are phase space limited if not kinematically prohibited,
the information of their amplitudes can still be useful, as the
T
in these modes can be a virtual particle involved in a decay of a heavy particle, such as a
B
meson, to multi-particle final states.

As noted previously for flavor exotic states and all other states in
{\bf 6}
the
T\to DS
decay amplitudes in the first scenario can only
have topological amplitudes
C_{S}
and
C_{DS}
, as depicted in Fig.
3
(a) and (b).
Although we have two different topological amplitudes contributing to the decay amplitudes of
T
in
{\bf 6}
,
the amplitudes are proportional to a single combination
2C_{S}+C_{DS}
as shown in Table
5
.
Consequently, their decay amplitudes are highly related.
This can be easily understood as in the
T\to DP
case,
since there is only one
{\bf 6}
in
\bar{\bf 3}\otimes({\bf 8}\oplus{\bf 1})
, see Eq. (
138
),
from the final state
DS
that can match the SU(3) quantum number of these
T
.

The sizes of the amplitudes of the following decay modes of flavor exotic states,

T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{+}\kappa^{+},D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{+}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{0}
,

T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{0}\kappa^{0},D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{-}

and

T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{0}\overline{\kappa}^{0},D^{+}\kappa^{-}
,
are identical, giving
|A|=|2C_{S}+C_{DS}|
,
and factor
1/\sqrt{2}
are needed for
|A|
of
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{0}\overline{\kappa}^{+}
and
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{+}\kappa^{0}
decays.
The flavor exotic nature of most of these states is self-evident from the table.
For example, the
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{0}
decay reveals the exotic quantum number of
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
.

For
T
in
{\bf 3}
, namely
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c}
,
T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c}
and
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}
,
there is only one combination, namely
C_{DS}-2C_{S}+2ACC_{S}
, in the amplitudes of
T\to Da_{0},D\kappa,D\bar{\kappa}
decays,
while another combination,
C_{S}+C_{DS}-3ACC_{D}-ACC_{S}
, is needed in
T\to Df_{0},D\sigma
decays
as there are two
\bar{\bf 3}
in
\bar{\bf 3}\otimes({\bf 8}\oplus{\bf 1})
, see Eq. (
138
),
from the final states that can match the SU(3) quantum number of these
T^{\prime\prime}_{c}
and
T^{\prime\prime}_{c\bar{s}}
.

The situation in scenario II is slightly different.
In this case, we only have one topological amplitude,
C_{DS}
, contributing to the multiplet containing flavor exotic states, namely
\overline{\bf 15}
,
as noted previously,
C_{S}
is not allowed in this scenario.
Here, the SU(3) quantum number of the final state
DS
is
\bar{\bf 3}\otimes({\bf 8}\oplus{\bf 1})
,
but these is only one
\overline{\bf 15}
in
\bar{\bf 3}\otimes({\bf 8}\oplus{\bf 1})
, see Eq. (
138
), that can match the SU(3) quantum number of these
T
.
Consequently, the
T\to DS
amplitudes of these states are highly related.
In particular, as shown in Table
6
, the following decay modes of flavor exotic states,

T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{+}\kappa^{+}
,

D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{+}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{0}
,

T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}\to D^{0}\kappa^{0}
,

D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{-}
,

T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{0}\overline{\kappa}^{0}
,

D^{+}\kappa^{-}
,
all have identical amplitude,
A=C_{DS}
.
Furthermore, the decay amplitudes of flavor exotic states,

T^{+}_{cs}\to D^{+}\overline{\kappa}^{0}
,

T^{-}_{cs}\to D^{0}\kappa^{-}
,

T^{++}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}\kappa^{+}
,

T^{+}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}\kappa^{0}
,

T^{++}_{c}\to D^{+}a_{0}^{+}

and

T^{-}_{c}\to D^{0}a_{0}^{-}
decays have
A=\sqrt{2}C_{DS}
,
and the amplitudes of

T^{+}_{c}\to D^{0}a_{0}^{+}
,

T^{+}_{c}\to D^{+}a_{0}^{0}
,

T^{0}_{c}\to D^{0}a_{0}^{0}

and

T^{0}_{c}\to D^{+}a_{0}^{-}

decays have
A\propto C_{DS}
with some simple proportional constants.
The flavor exotic nature of most of these states is self-evident from the table.
For
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
the
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\to D_{s}^{+}a_{0}^{0}
decay reveals the exotic nature of
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
,
while for
T^{+}_{c}
and
T^{0}_{c}
, more works are required.
For example, one needs to verify that they do not decay to
Df_{0}
and
D\sigma
.

For
T
in
{\bf 3}
, namely
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c}
,
T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c}
and
T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}
,
there is only one combination, namely
C_{DS}+4ACC_{S}
, in the amplitudes of
T\to Da_{0},D\kappa,D\bar{\kappa}
decays,
while another combination,
C_{DS}+ACC_{S}+3ACC_{D}
, is needed in
T\to Df_{0},D\sigma
decays
as there are two
\bar{\bf 3}
in
\bar{\bf 3}\otimes({\bf 8}\oplus{\bf 1})
from the final states that can match the SU(3) quantum number of these
T^{\prime\prime}_{c}
and
T^{\prime\prime}_{c\bar{s}}
.

IV
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
and
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays

IV.1
Topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
and
\overline{D}T
decays

IV.1.1
Topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decays

The
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decays in
\Delta S=-1
transitions are governed by the a tree operator
(\bar{s}c)_{V-A}(\bar{c}b)_{V-A}
and a penguin operator
(\bar{c}c)(\bar{s}b)
.
These operators have the same flavor structure that can be expressed as
(\bar{q}_{j}H^{j}c)\,(\bar{c}b)
with
\bar{q}_{j}=(\bar{u},\,\bar{d},\,\bar{s})
and

H=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
0\\
1\end{array}\right).

(33)

Consequently, in scenario I the Hamiltonian governing the
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}(\bar{c}\bar{q}[q^{\prime}q^{\prime\prime}])
decays is given by

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\bar{T}}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{D}^{m}H^{j}T^{i}_{[ji]}+C_{\bar{T}}\,\overline{B}_{m}T^{m}_{[jk]}H^{j}\overline{D}^{k}+E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{j}H^{j}T^{i}_{[ik]}\overline{D}^{k}

(34)

\displaystyle+P_{\bar{T},1}\,\overline{B}_{m}T^{m}_{[jk]}H^{j}\overline{D}^{k}+P_{\bar{T},2}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{D}^{m}H^{j}T^{i}_{[ji]}+PA_{\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{j}H^{j}T^{i}_{[ik]}\overline{D}^{k},

while in scenario II the Hamiltonian for the
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}(\bar{c}\bar{q}\{q^{\prime}q^{\prime\prime}\})
decays is given by,

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\bar{T}}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{D}^{m}H^{j}T^{i}_{\{ji\}}+C_{\bar{T}}\,\overline{B}_{m}T^{m}_{\{jk\}}H^{j}\overline{D}^{k}+E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{j}H^{j}T^{i}_{\{ik\}}\overline{D}^{k}

(35)

\displaystyle+P_{\bar{T},1}\,\overline{B}_{m}T^{m}_{\{jk\}}H^{j}\overline{D}^{k}+P_{\bar{T},2}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{D}^{m}H^{j}T^{i}_{\{ji\}}+PA_{\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{j}H^{j}T^{i}_{\{ik\}}\overline{D}^{k}.

For notational simplicity, we use the same notations on topological amplitudes in both scenarios, without any assumption on their equality.
There are three tree topological amplitudes,
T_{\bar{T}}
,
C_{\bar{T}}
and
E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}

and three penguin topological amplitudes,
P_{\bar{T},1}
,
P_{\bar{T},2}
and
PA_{\bar{B}}
,
as depicted in Fig.
4
.
These
T_{\bar{T}}
,
C_{\bar{T}}
and
E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}
amplitudes denote external
W
, internal
W
and exchange amplitudes, respectively,
where the subscripts denote the particles receiving the
\bar{c}s
quarks from the
W
line in the
b\to cW^{-},W^{-}\to\bar{c}s
transition.
For
P_{\bar{T},1}
,
P_{\bar{T},2}
and
PA_{\bar{B}}
amplitudes, they are penguin and penguin-annihilation amplitudes,
where the subscripts denote the particles receiving the
s
quark from the
b\to s\bar{c}c
transition.

Figure 4:
Topological diagrams of
(a)
C_{\bar{T}}
(internal
W
), (b)
T_{\bar{T}}
(external
W
), (c)
E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}
(exchange),
(d)
P_{\bar{T},1}
(penguin), (e)
P_{\bar{T},2}
(penguin)and (f)
PA_{\bar{B}}
(penguin annihilation)
amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decays.
These are flavor flow diagrams. Only (a) and (d) contribute to modes with flavor exotic states or other states in the same multiplets.

The relative sizes of these topological amplitudes are expected to be

\displaystyle|T_{\bar{T}}|>|C_{\bar{T}}|>|P_{\bar{T},1}|,|P_{\bar{T},2}|,|E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}|,|PA_{\bar{B}}|.

(36)

Although the external
W
diagram is expected to dominate, it is unable to produce a flavor exotic
\overline{T}
in the final state, as the
q\bar{q}
pair is flavor connected, see Fig.
4
(b).
These flavor exotic
\overline{T}
and other states in the same multiplets, namely
{\bf 6}
in scenario I and
\overline{\bf 15}
in scenario II, only have contribution from
(C_{\bar{T}}+P_{\bar{T},1})
,
1
1

1

These two tpological amplitudes can only occur in this combination, see Eqs. (
34
) and (
35
).
and, consequently, their
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decay amplitudes are highly related.

The above effective Hamiltonians are for the
\Delta S=-1
transition,
but they can be easily transformed into those for the
\Delta S=0
transition, by simply replacing
H
in Eq. (
33
) with

H^{\prime}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0\\
1\\
0\end{array}\right),

(37)

and with

T_{\bar{T}}
,

C_{\bar{T}}
,

E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}
,

P_{\bar{T},1}
,

P_{\bar{T},2}
,

PA_{\bar{B}}

replaced with

T^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}
,

C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}
,

E^{\prime}_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}
,

P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1}
,

P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},2}
,

PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}}

respectively.
It is understood that these amplitudes are related by Cabibbo–Kobayashi–Maskawa (CKM) matrix elements, giving

T^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}=\frac{V^{*}_{cd}}{V^{*}_{cs}}T_{\bar{T}}
,

C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}=\frac{V^{*}_{cd}}{V^{*}_{cs}}C_{\bar{T}}
,

E^{\prime}_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}=\frac{V^{*}_{cd}}{V^{*}_{cs}}E_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}
,

P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1}=\frac{V^{*}_{td}}{V^{*}_{ts}}P_{\bar{T},1}
,

P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},2}=\frac{V^{*}_{td}}{V^{*}_{ts}}P_{\bar{T},2}

and

PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}}=\frac{V^{*}_{td}}{V^{*}_{ts}}PA_{\bar{B}}
.

Figure 5:
Topological diagrams of
(a)
C_{\bar{D}T}
(internal
W
), (b)
T_{\bar{D}}
(external
W
), (c)
E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}
(exchange),
(d)
P_{T}
(penguin), (e)
P_{\bar{D}}
(penguin) and (f)
PA_{\bar{B}}
(penguin annihilation)
amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays.
These are flavor flow diagrams. Only (a) and (d) contribute to modes with flavor exotic states or other states in the same multiplets.

IV.1.2
Topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays

We turn to
\overline{B}_{q}\to\overline{D}T
decays.
The tree
b\to cW^{-},W^{-}\to\bar{c}s
and the penguin
b\to s\bar{c}c
transitions can also induce
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T(cq[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}])
and
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T(cq\{\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}\})
decays as well.
The Hamiltonian governing the
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T(cq[\bar{q}^{\prime}\bar{q}^{\prime\prime}])
decays in
\Delta S=-1
transitions in scenario I is given by

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\bar{D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{[mi]}H^{j}D_{j}+C_{\bar{D}T}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{j}\overline{T}_{j}^{[mk]}D_{k}+E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}\overline{T}_{j}^{[jk]}D_{k}

(38)

\displaystyle+P_{T}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{j}\overline{T}_{j}^{[mk]}D_{k}+P_{\bar{D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{[mi]}H^{j}D_{j}+PA_{\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}\overline{T}_{j}^{[jk]}D_{k},

while the one for the
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}(\bar{c}\bar{q}\{q^{\prime}q^{\prime\prime}\})
decays in scenario II is given by,

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\bar{D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{\{mi\}}H^{j}D_{j}+C_{\bar{D}T}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{j}\overline{T}_{j}^{\{mk\}}D_{k}+E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}\overline{T}_{j}^{\{jk\}}D_{k}

(39)

\displaystyle+P_{T}\,\overline{B}_{m}H^{j}\overline{T}_{j}^{\{mk\}}D_{k}+P_{\bar{D}}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{\{mi\}}H^{j}D_{j}+PA_{\bar{B}}\,\overline{B}_{i}H^{i}\overline{T}_{j}^{\{jk\}}D_{k}.

For notational simplicity, we use the same notations on topological amplitudes in both scenarios.
There are three tree topological amplitudes,
T_{\bar{D}}
,
C_{\bar{D}T}
and
E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}

and three penguin topological amplitudes,
P_{T}
,
P_{\bar{D}}
and
PA_{\bar{B}}
,
and
they are depicted in Fig.
5
.
These
T_{\bar{D}}
,
C_{\bar{D}T}
and
E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}
amplitudes denote external
W
, internal
W
and exchange amplitudes, respectively, and the subscripts denote the particle(s) receiving the
\bar{c}s
quarks from the
W
line in the
b\to cW^{-},W^{-}\to\bar{c}s
transition.
For
P_{T}
,
P_{\bar{D}}
and
PA_{\bar{B}}
amplitudes, they are penguin and penguin-annihilation amplitudes,
where the subscripts denote the particles receiving the
s
quark from the
b\to s\bar{c}c
transition.
The effective Hamiltonians governing
\Delta S=0
transitions can be obtained from the above equations with
H
replaced with
H^{\prime}
given in Eq. (
37
),
and with
T_{\bar{D}},C_{\bar{D}T},E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}
,
P_{T}
,
P_{\bar{D}}
and
PA_{\bar{B}}
replaced with

T^{\prime}_{\bar{D}},C^{\prime}_{\bar{D}},E^{\prime}_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}
,
P^{\prime}_{T}
,
P^{\prime}_{\bar{D}}
and
PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}}
, respectively.
It is understood that these amplitudes are related by CKM matrix elements, giving

T^{\prime}_{\bar{D}}=\frac{V^{*}_{cd}}{V^{*}_{cs}}T_{\bar{D}}
,

C^{\prime}_{\bar{D}T}=\frac{V^{*}_{cd}}{V^{*}_{cs}}C_{\bar{D}T}
,

E^{\prime}_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}=\frac{V^{*}_{cd}}{V^{*}_{cs}}E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}
,

P^{\prime}_{\bar{D}}=\frac{V^{*}_{td}}{V^{*}_{ts}}P_{\bar{D}}
,

P^{\prime}_{T}=\frac{V^{*}_{td}}{V^{*}_{ts}}P_{T}

and

PA^{\prime}_{\bar{B}}=\frac{V^{*}_{td}}{V^{*}_{ts}}PA_{\bar{B}}
.

The relative sizes of the tree topological amplitudes are expected to be

\displaystyle|T_{\bar{D}}|>|C_{\bar{D}T}|>|P_{T}|,|P_{\bar{D}}|,|E_{\bar{D}\bar{B}}|,|PA_{\bar{B}}|.

(40)

Although the external
W
diagram is expected to dominate, it is unable to produce a flavor exotic
T
, as the
q\bar{q}
pair in the diagram, see Fig.
5
(b), is flavor connected.
The
\overline{B}_{q}\to\overline{D}T
decay amplitudes of modes involving
T
in
{\bf 6}
in scenario I or
\overline{\bf 15}
in scenario II only have contribution from
(C_{\bar{D}T}+P_{T})
,
2
2

2

These two tpological amplitudes can only occur in this combination, see Eqs. (
38
) and (
39
).
i.e. the diagrams depicted in
5
(a) and (d). Consequently, the amplitudes of these states are highly related.

From the above discussion, we may wrongly have the impression that a
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decay is similar to a
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decay.
In fact, by comparing Fig.
5
to Fig.
4
, one can easily see the differences.
For example,
in
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decays, the external
W
diagram,
T_{\bar{T}}
, has a
\overline{T}
emitted from
W^{-}
, as shown in Fig.
4
(b), while
in
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays, the emitted particle is
\overline{D}
instead, see Fig.
5
(b).
Furthermore, there is no counterpart of the internal
W
diagram
C_{\bar{D}T}
, Fig.
5
(a), in
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decays,
as the
C_{\bar{T}}
in Fig.
4
(a) is different.
Explicitly, the former has
\bar{D}T
sharing the
c\bar{q}
from
W
, while in the latter, both
c\bar{q}
go to
\overline{T}
.
Consequently, one cannot identify the topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
to those in
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays.

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}}

-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})

\overline{11^{\prime}}^{*}

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}}

-\sqrt{\frac{2}{3}}(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})

\overline{13^{\prime}}

B^{-}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,\prime-}_{\bar{c}}

\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})

\overline{13^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime-}_{\bar{c}}

\frac{1}{\sqrt{3}}(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})

\overline{13^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime-}_{\bar{c}}

-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})

\overline{14^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,\prime 0}_{\bar{c}}

\frac{1}{2\sqrt{3}}(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})

\overline{15^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime-}_{\bar{c}s}

\frac{1}{2}(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})

\overline{16^{\prime}}

B^{-}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}}

\frac{1}{2}(4(T^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},2})+(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1}))

\overline{16^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}}

2(T^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},2})+(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})+2(E^{\prime}_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}+PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}})

\overline{16^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime 0}_{\bar{c}}

\frac{1}{2}(4(T^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},2})+(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1}))

\overline{17^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime 0}_{\bar{c}}

\frac{1}{2}((C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})+4(E^{\prime}_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}+PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}}))

\overline{18^{\prime}}

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}s}

\frac{1}{2}((C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})+4(E^{\prime}_{\bar{T}\bar{B}}+PE^{\prime}_{\bar{B}}))

The
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decay amplitudes decomposed in these topological amplitudes are shown in Table
7
for
\Delta S=-1
and
\Delta S=0
transitions in scenario I and in Table
8
in scenario II.
Note that these results also hold by replacing
D
by
D^{*}
.

From Table
7
we see that decay modes involving flavor exotic
\overline{T}
in scenario I are governed by
(C_{\bar{T}}+P_{\bar{T},1})
,
and, consequently, their decay rates are highly related.
Furthermore, as shown in Table
7
all twelve modes with
T
in
{\bf 6}
are highly related, as they are either proportional to
(C_{\bar{T}}+P_{\bar{T},1})
or
(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})
.
In particular, the following modes involving flavor exotic states have relations on rates,

\displaystyle\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}s})=2\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}s})=2\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}s})

(41)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}s})=2\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}\bar{s}}),

where the approximation in
``\simeq"
is neglecting the penguin contributions.
Note that all four flavor exotic states in scenario I are involved in these
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decays,
but we need decay modes in
\Delta S=-1
and
\Delta S=0
transitions.

One can roughly estimate the error in the above approximation by using

\displaystyle\frac{|P_{\bar{T},1}|}{|C_{\bar{T}}|}\sim\left|\frac{V_{tb}V^{*}_{ts}(c_{4}+\frac{c_{3}}{3})}{V_{cb}V^{*}_{cs}(c_{2}+\frac{c_{1}}{3})}\right|\simeq 0.19,

(42)

and

\displaystyle\frac{|P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1}|}{|C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}|}\sim\left|\frac{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}(c_{4}+\frac{c_{3}}{3})}{V_{cb}V^{*}_{cd}(c_{2}+\frac{c_{1}}{3})}\right|\simeq 0.17,

(43)

where
c_{i}
are the Wilson coefficients, see
Beneke:2001ev
.
Therefore, the errors of neglecting penguin contributions are roughly estimated to be of the order
\lambda
.
To effectively control the penguin pollution, further work is needed; see, for example, a study in a similar situation
Fleischer:2007zn
,
where information of direct CP violation is needed.

We turn to scenario II.
Similarly all twenty four modes with
T
in
\overline{\bf 15}
are highly related,
as they are either proportional to
(C_{\bar{T}}+P_{\bar{T},1})
or
(C^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}+P^{\prime}_{\bar{T},1})
.
In particular, for the following fourteen modes involving flavor exotic states in scenario II, we have

\displaystyle 2\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}s})=4\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}s})=2\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}s})

(44)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(B^{-}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}ss})=\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}ss})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle 2\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}s})=4\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}\bar{s}})=2\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}\bar{s}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}})=3\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}})=3\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}}),

where the approximation in
``\simeq"
is neglecting the penguin contributions.
Note that even with both
\Delta S=-1
and
\Delta S=0
transitions, we only have nine out of twelve flavor exotic states involved in these
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
decays.
On the other hand, the above relations impose strong restrictions on these rates.
The continue unobservation of some of these modes, such as
B^{-}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}ss}
and
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}ss}
decays, will certainly pose tension to scenario II.

The
B^{-}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}s}

and
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}s}
decays
correspond to the
B^{-}\to D^{+}\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{\,--}

and
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{0}
decays, respectively, in both scenarios.
These modes were
reported by LHCb in the observation of
\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{\,--}
and
\overline{T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}}(2900)^{0}

in
B^{-}\to D^{+}D_{s}^{-}\pi^{-}
and
\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}D_{s}^{-}\pi^{+}
decays
LHCb:2022sfr
;
LHCb:2022lzp
.
Note that the
\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}\bar{s}}
decay involving
\overline{T^{*}_{cs0}}(2870)^{0}
has not been observed yet, as it is a CKM suppressed
\Delta S=-1
transition.
It will be interesting to search for this mode to check whether these three states belong to the same multiplet or not.

Modes in
\overline{\bf 3}
or
\overline{\bf 3}^{\prime}
are also related.
From Table
7
and Table
8
, one can infer the following relations on rates in both scenarios,

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}})=\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime 0}_{\bar{c}})\simeq\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}s}),

(45)

\displaystyle\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}s})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}s})\simeq\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}})

(46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}}),

and

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}s})\simeq\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,\prime\prime-}_{\bar{c}}),

(47)

where the approximation is neglecting penguin contributions.
The rates in Eqs. (
46
) and (
47
) are expected to be larger than those in Eq. (
45
) in the same
\Delta S
transition. as the last two sets have contributions from external
W
amplitudes,
T_{\bar{T}}
or
T^{\prime}_{\bar{T}}
.

Note that in ref.
Qin:2022nof
the amplitudes of the following four modes in
\Delta S=-1
transitions in scenario II, namely

B^{-}\to D^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}s}
,

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{0}\overline{T}^{0}_{\bar{c}s}
,

B^{-}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,--}_{\bar{c}ss}
,

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{+}\overline{T}^{\,-}_{\bar{c}ss}
,
were also given.
Their results agree with those shown in Table
8
, but with
(C_{\bar{T}}+P_{\bar{T},1})
replaced with
CV_{cb}V^{*}_{cs}
,
while the rest in Table
8
and all in Table
7
are new.

The final state
\overline{T}
can further decay to
\bar{D}P
and
\bar{D}S
, or they can connect to these states virtually.
One can obtain their full amplitudes using results in the section and those in Sec.
III
.

From Table

we see that the decay modes involving
T
in
{\bf 6}
in scenario I
are governed by
(C_{\bar{D}T}+P_{T})
or
(C^{\prime}_{\bar{D}T}+P^{\prime}_{T})
.
Consequently, their decay rates are highly related.
In particular, the modes involving flavor exotic states have the following relations on decay rates,

\displaystyle\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{0}_{cs})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{0}_{cs})\simeq\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{0}_{c\bar{s}})

(48)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{0}_{c\bar{s}})=2\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}),

where the approximation in
``\simeq"
is neglecting the penguin contributions.
Note that three out of four flavor exotic states, namely
T^{0}_{cs}
,
T^{+}_{cs}
and
T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}
, are involved in these
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays,
while the other flavor exotic state,
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}
is absent.
The absence of the doubly charged state in
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays can be easily understood from charge conservation.
We need to consider both
\Delta S=-1
and
\Delta S=0
transitions in
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays to have both
T^{0}_{cs}
and
T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}
,
but without
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}
.

One can roughly estimate the error in the above approximation by using

\displaystyle\frac{|P_{T}|}{|C_{\bar{D}T}|}\sim\left|\frac{V_{tb}V^{*}_{ts}(c_{4}+\frac{c_{3}}{3})}{V_{cb}V^{*}_{cs}(c_{2}+\frac{c_{1}}{3})}\right|\simeq 0.19,

(49)

and

\displaystyle\frac{|P^{\prime}_{T}|}{|C^{\prime}_{\bar{D}T}|}\sim\left|\frac{V_{tb}V^{*}_{td}(c_{4}+\frac{c_{3}}{3})}{V_{cb}V^{*}_{cd}(c_{2}+\frac{c_{1}}{3})}\right|\simeq 0.17,

(50)

where
c_{i}
are the Wilson coefficients, see
Beneke:2001ev
.
Therefore, the errors of neglecting penguin contributions are roughly estimated to be of the order
\lambda
.
To effectively control the penguin pollution, further work is needed; see, for example, a study in a somewhat similar situation
Fleischer:2007zn
,
where information of direct CP violation is needed.

We turn to scenario II.
Likewise, from Table
10
,
the decay modes involving
T
in
\overline{\bf 15}
only have contributions from
(C_{\bar{D}T}+P_{T})
or
(C^{\prime}_{\bar{D}T}+P^{\prime}_{T})
, and, consequently, their amplitudes are highly related.
In particular, the modes involving flavor exotic
T
have the following relations on decay rates,

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{+}_{cs})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{0}_{cs})=2\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{0}_{cs})=\Gamma(B^{-}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{-}_{cs})

(51)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle 2\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{0}_{c\bar{s}})=2\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{0}_{c\bar{s}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}})=4\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{+}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}})=3\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{+}_{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{0}_{c})=3\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{0}_{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{-}_{c}),

where the approximation in
``\simeq"
is neglecting the penguin contributions.
We have nine out of twelve flavor exotic states involved in these
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays,
while all doubly charged states,
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}
,
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}\bar{s}}
and
T^{++}_{c}
, are absent,
as
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T^{++}
decays are forbiden by charge conservation.

Note that the
B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{0}_{cs}
decay
corresponds to the
B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
decay
reported by LHCb in the observation of
T_{cs0}(2870)^{0}
,
from
B^{-}\to D^{-}D^{+}K^{-}
decay with
B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0},T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}\to D^{+}K^{-}
decay
LHCb:2020pxc
.
Recently, LHCb also reports the observation of
B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0},T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}\to D^{0}K^{0}_{S}

in
B^{-}\to D^{-}D^{0}K^{0}_{S}
decay
LHCb:2024xyx
agreeing with

\displaystyle\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{0}_{cs},T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{+}K^{-})=\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{0}_{cs},T^{0}_{cs}\to D^{0}\overline{K}^{0}),

(52)

which can be inferred from Tables
3
and
4
in scenarios I and II, respectively.

Note that the
\overline{B}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{0}_{cs}
decay has not been observed yet. It will be useful to search for this mode and check the relations in Eqs. (
48
) and (
51
).

Modes in
\overline{\bf 3}
or
\overline{\bf 3}^{\prime}
are also related.
From Table
7
and Table
8
, one can infer the following relations on rates in both scenarios,

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(B^{-}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c})\simeq\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to D^{-}T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c})

(53)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}),

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}})\simeq\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c}),

(54)

and

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c})\simeq\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to\overline{D}^{0}T^{\prime\prime 0}_{c})

(55)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{cs}}{V_{cd}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D_{s}^{-}T^{\prime\prime+}_{c\bar{s}}),

where the approximation is neglecting penguin contributions.
As they have contributions from external
W
amplitudes,
T_{\bar{D}}
or
T^{\prime}_{\bar{D}}
,
the rates in Eqs. (
53
) and (
54
) are expected to be larger than those in Eq. (
55
) in the same
\Delta S
transition.

\Delta S=-1

\overline{B}{}^{0}\to D^{-}T^{+}_{cs}
,

were also given.

, but with
(C_{\bar{D}T}+P_{T})
replaced with

,
while the rest in Table

and all in Table

are new.

The final state
T
can further decay to
DP
and
DS
, or they can connect to those states virtually.
One can obtain their full amplitudes using results in the section and those in Sec.
III
.

Before we end this section,
it will be useful to compare the participation of
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{++}
,
T^{*}_{c\bar{s}0}(2900)^{0}
and
T^{*}_{cs0}(2870)^{0}

in
\overline{B}\to D\overline{T}
and
\overline{B}\to\overline{D}T
decays in
\Delta S=-1
and
\Delta S=0
transitions.
We show in Table
11
such a comparison with the relevant topological amplitudes listed.
It is evident that using only
\Delta S=-1
transitions, one is unable to check if these three states belong to the same multiplet or not, as
(C_{\bar{T}}+P_{\bar{T},1})
and
(C_{\bar{D}T}+P_{T})
are unrelated.
Therefore, to verify that, in any case, we need to consider the CKM suppressed
\Delta S=0
transitions as well.

V
\overline{B}\to TP
and
\overline{B}\to TS
decays

V.1
Topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to TP
and
TS
decays

V.1.1
Topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to TP
decays

The
(\bar{d}u)(\bar{c}b)
operator in the weak Hamiltonian

H_{\rm W}
can be expressed as
(\bar{q}_{i}H^{i}_{j}q^{j})\,(\bar{c}b)
, where
q^{j}=(u,\,d,\,s)
and

H=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
1&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right).

(56)

Consequently, for
\overline{B}\to T_{cq[q^{\prime}q^{\prime\prime}]}P
decays in scenario I, we have the following effective Hamiltonian,

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{P}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{[mk]}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{j}_{i}+C_{T}\,\overline{B}_{m}\Pi^{m}_{k}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{i}^{[jk]}

(57)

\displaystyle+C_{PT,1}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{[mj]}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{k}_{i}+C_{PT,2}\,\overline{B}_{m}\Pi^{m}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{k}^{[jk]}

\displaystyle+C_{TP}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{[mk]}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{j}_{k}+E_{\bar{B}T}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{m}^{[jk]}\Pi^{m}_{k}

\displaystyle+E_{\bar{B}P}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{j}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{[mk]},

while for
\overline{B}\to T_{cq\{q^{\prime}q^{\prime\prime}\}}P
decays in scenario II, we have

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{P}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{\{mk\}}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{j}_{i}+C_{T}\,\overline{B}_{m}\Pi^{m}_{k}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{i}^{\{jk\}}

(58)

\displaystyle+C_{PT,1}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{\{mj\}}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{k}_{i}+C_{PT,2}\,\overline{B}_{m}\Pi^{m}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{k}^{\{jk\}}

\displaystyle+C_{TP}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{\{mk\}}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{j}_{k}+E_{\bar{B}T}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{m}^{\{jk\}}\Pi^{m}_{k}

\displaystyle+E_{\bar{B}P}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\Pi^{j}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{\{mk\}}.

For notational simplicity, we do not distinguish the topological amplitudes in scenarios I and II.
The above construction also applies to
\overline{B}\to TV
decays by simple substitution.
Note that in the above effective Hamiltonians, we do not consider terms with
\Pi^{i}_{i}
.
In principle, they can take place, but these diagrams are more detached and are expected to be suppressed.
Furthermore, the addition of these terms only affects
\eta_{1}
.
We will neglect them in this work.
The effect of this approximation is that we do not differentiate
\eta_{1}
from
\eta_{8}
, and one may view this as a nonet symmetry.

There are seven topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to TP
decays, namely

C_{T}
(internal
W
),

C_{PT,1}
(internal
W
),

C_{TP}
(internal
W
),

E_{\bar{B}T}
(exchange),

T_{P}
(external
W
),

C_{PT,2}
(internal
W
)
and

E_{\bar{B}P}
(exchange)
topological amplitudes.
They are depicted in Fig.
6
.
The subscripts of these topological amplitudes indicate the final state meson(s) receiving the
q_{i}\bar{q}^{j}
quarks
from the
W
line in the
b\to cW^{-},W^{-}\to q_{i}\bar{q}^{j}
transition.
For example.
C_{TP}
indicates
T
receiving the
q_{i}
while
P
receiving the
\bar{q}^{j}
, see Fig.
6
(c).
It is evident that when both are received by a single meson, as in Fig.
6
(a), one simply indicates that meson in the subscript, as in
C_{T}
.

Figure 6:
Topological diagrams of
(a)
C_{T}
,
(b)
C_{PT,1}
,
(c)
C_{TP}
,
(d)
E_{\bar{B}T}
,
(e)
T_{P}
,
(f)
C_{PT,2}

and
(g)
E_{\bar{B}P}

amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to TP
decays, with
T
,
C
and
E
as external
W
,
internal
W
and exchange amplitudes, respectively.
The subscripts indicate the final state meson(s) receiving the
q_{i}\bar{q}^{j}
quarks from the
W
line in the
b\to cW^{-},W^{-}\to q_{i}\bar{q}^{j}
transition.
Only diagrams (a) to (d) contribute to modes involving flavor exotic states.

The above effective Hamiltonians are for the
\Delta S=0
transition, and they can be easily transformed into those for the
\Delta S=-1
transition, by simply replacing
H
in Eq. (
56
) with

H^{\prime}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
1&0&0\end{array}\right).

(59)

and with

T_{P}
,
C_{T}
,
C_{PT,1}
,
C_{PT,2}
,
C_{TP}
,
E_{\bar{B}T}
,
E_{\bar{B}P}
replaced with

T^{\prime}_{P}
,
C^{\prime}_{T}
,
C^{\prime}_{PT,1}
,
C^{\prime}_{PT,2}
,
C^{\prime}_{TP}
,
E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}T}
,
E^{\prime}_{\bar{B},P,2}
, respectively.
It is understood that these amplitudes are related by CKM matrix elements giving

T^{\prime}_{P}=\frac{V^{*}_{us}}{V^{*}_{ud}}T_{P}
,

C^{\prime}_{T}=\frac{V^{*}_{us}}{V^{*}_{ud}}C_{T}
,

C^{\prime}_{PT,1}=\frac{V^{*}_{us}}{V^{*}_{ud}}C_{PT,1}
,

C^{\prime}_{PT,2}=\frac{V^{*}_{us}}{V^{*}_{ud}}C_{PT,2}
,

C^{\prime}_{TP}=\frac{V^{*}_{us}}{V^{*}_{ud}}C_{TP}
,

E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}T}=\frac{V^{*}_{us}}{V^{*}_{ud}}E_{\bar{B}T}

and

E^{\prime}_{\bar{B}P}=\frac{V^{*}_{us}}{V^{*}_{ud}}E_{\bar{B}P}
.

Although we have seven topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to TP
decays,
only four topological amplitudes, namely
C^{(\prime)}_{T}
,
C^{(\prime)}_{PT,1}
,
C^{(\prime)}_{TP}
and
E^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}P}
,
contribute to modes involving flavor exotic states and other states of the same multiplets.
As one can see from Fig.
6
(a) to (d), only these four topological amplitudes do not have flavor-connected
q\bar{q}
pairs in tetraquarks.
All other diagrams necessarily involve flavor-connected
q\bar{q}
pairs in tetraquarks, and they are “traceful”, which, as a result, cannot produce “traceless” tetraquarks.

Topological amplitudes in
\overline{B}\to TS
decays

,

as external

,

Only diagrams (a) to (h) contribute to modes involving flavor exotic states.
Diagrams (f) and (g) are absent in scenario II.

For
\overline{B}\to T_{cq[q^{\prime}q^{\prime\prime}]}S
decays in
\Delta S=0
transitions in scenario I, we have

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{S}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{[mk]}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{pi}+C_{S}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{p}^{[mk]}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{ki}

(60)

\displaystyle+C_{T}\,\overline{B}_{m}S^{mp}_{pk}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{i}^{[jk]}+C_{ST,1}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{[mj]}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{kp}_{pi}

\displaystyle+C_{ST,2}\,\overline{B}_{m}S^{mp}_{ki}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{p}^{[jk]}+C_{ST,3}\,\overline{B}_{m}S^{mp}_{pi}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{k}^{[jk]}

\displaystyle+C_{TS,1}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{[mk]}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{pk}+C_{TS,2}\,\overline{B}_{m}S^{mj}_{kp}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{i}^{[pk]}

\displaystyle+E_{\bar{B}S,2}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{pm}\overline{T}_{k}^{[mk]}+E_{\bar{B}S,1}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{km}\overline{T}_{p}^{[mk]}

\displaystyle+E_{\bar{B}T}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{p}^{[jk]}S^{mp}_{km}

while for
\overline{B}\to T_{cq\{q^{\prime}q^{\prime\prime}\}}S
decays in scenario II, we have

\displaystyle H_{\rm eff}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{S}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{\{mk\}}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{pi}+C_{S}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{p}^{\{mk\}}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{ki}

(61)

\displaystyle+C_{T}\,\overline{B}_{m}S^{mp}_{pk}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{i}^{\{jk\}}+C_{ST,1}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{k}^{\{mj\}}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{kp}_{pi}

\displaystyle+C_{ST,2}\,\overline{B}_{m}S^{mp}_{ki}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{p}^{\{jk\}}+C_{ST,3}\,\overline{B}_{m}S^{mp}_{pi}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{k}^{\{jk\}}

\displaystyle+C_{TS,1}\,\overline{B}_{m}\overline{T}_{i}^{\{mk\}}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{pk}+E_{\bar{B}S,2}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,S^{jp}_{pm}\overline{T}_{k}^{\{mk\}}

\displaystyle+E_{\bar{B}T}\,\overline{B}_{i}\,H^{i}_{j}\,\overline{T}_{p}^{\{jk\}}S^{mp}_{km}.

For notational simplicity, we do not distinguish the topological amplitudes in scenarios I and II.
Note that in the above effective Hamiltonians, we do not consider terms with
S^{mp}_{pm}
.
In principle, they can take place, but these diagrams are more detached and are expected to be small.
For practical purposes, we will neglect them in this work, and this approximation only affects modes with
\sigma
and
f_{0}
.
One may view this as we use nonet symmetry.

In scenario I we have eleven topological amplitudes, namely

C_{S}
,

C_{T}
,

C_{ST,1}
,

C_{ST,2}
,

C_{TS,1}
,

C_{TS,2}

E_{\bar{B}S,1}
,

E_{\bar{B}T}
,

T_{S}
,

C_{ST,3}
,
and
E_{\bar{B}S,2}
,
in
\overline{B}\to TS
decays,
with
T
,
C
and
E
as external
W
,
internal
W
and exchange amplitudes, respectively.
They are depicted in Fig.
7
.
This is the case where we have the largest set of topological amplitudes.
As in the
\overline{B}\to TP
case, the subscript denotes the meson(s) where the
q^{i}\bar{q}_{j}
, from the
b\to cW^{-}\to cq^{i}\bar{q}_{j}
transition, contribute to.
For example,
C_{ST,1}
meaning
S
receives the
q^{i}
, while
T
receives the
\bar{q}_{j}
.
Note that there are only nine topological amplitudes in scenario II.
This can be understood as follows.
The light quarks in
S
are antisymmetric, while in scenario II the anti-quarks in
T
are symmetric.
Hence, when these pairs are fully connected, as in Fig.
7
(f) and (g), the corresponding topological amplitudes, namely
C_{TS,2}

and
E_{\bar{B}S,1}
, vanish.
Furthermore, only diagrams in Fig.
7
(a) to (h) contribute to modes with flavor exotic states in scenario I and with those in the same multiplets, while only those in Fig.
7
(a) to (e) and (h) contribute to those modes in scenario II.
However, as we shall see, the numbers of independent combinations of these topological amplitudes in these modes are smaller.

\overline{B}\to TP
and

V.2.1
\overline{B}\to TP
decay amplitudes

The
\overline{B}\to TP
decay amplitudes, decomposed in these topological amplitudes, are shown in Table
12
for
\Delta S=0
and
-1
transitions in scenario I, and in Tables
13
and
14
for
\Delta S=0
and
\Delta S=-1
transitions in scenario II, respectively.
These results can be applied to
\overline{B}\to TV
decays by replacing
P
with
V
suitably.

From Table
12
, we see that in scenario I, there are eight modes in
\Delta S=0
transitions and seven modes in
\Delta S=-1
transitions that involve flavor exotic states. Except
T^{++}_{c\bar{s}}
, all three other flavor exotic states are included, as a
\overline{B}\to T^{++}P
decay is prohibited by charge conservation.

The amplitudes are more complicated than in the previous cases, and one expect complex relations between rates.
Nevertheless, concerning modes with
T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}
,
T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}
and
T^{0}_{cs}
, we have several relations,

\displaystyle\Gamma(B^{-}\to T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}K^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{ud}}{V_{us}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(B^{-}\to T^{0}_{cs}\pi^{-})=2\left|\frac{V_{ud}}{V_{us}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to T^{0}_{cs}\pi^{0}),

(62)

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}\overline{K}{}^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\frac{V_{ud}}{V_{us}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to T^{0}_{cs}K^{0}),

(63)

and

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}^{0}\to T^{0}_{cs}K^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\left|\frac{V_{ud}}{V_{us}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}K^{-})=\left|\frac{V_{ud}}{V_{us}}\right|^{2}\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}\overline{K}^{0}),

(64)

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to T^{+}_{c\bar{s}}\pi^{-})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Gamma(\overline{B}{}_{s}^{0}\to T^{0}_{c\bar{s}}\pi^{0}).

(65)

These relations are useful in verifying the multiplet to which these three states belong.
In other words, if they belong to the same multiplet, the
{\bf 6}
in this case, their
\overline{B}\to TP
decays should satisfy the above relations.

The above relations can be understood as follows.
Since
\overline{B}
is a
\bar{\bf 3}
, the operator
(\bar{c}b)(\bar{d}u)
or
(\bar{c}b)(\bar{s}u)
is an
{\bf 8}
,
and we have
\bar{\bf 3}\times{\bf 8}=\overline{\bf 15}\oplus{\bf 6}\oplus\bar{\bf 3}
, as shown in Eq. (
138
).
On the other hand, the final states consist of
T
in
{\bf 6}
and
P=\pi,K
in
{\bf 8}
,
they form
{\bf 6}\otimes{\bf 8}
and there are one
\overline{\bf 15}
, one
{\bf 6}
and one
{\bf 3}
in
{\bf 6}\otimes{\bf 8}
, see Eq. (
139
),
that can match the SU(3) quantum numbers of
\overline{B}
and the operator.
As noted previously, only four out of eleven topological amplitudes can contribute to modes with flavor exotic states.
In fact, from the above argument we see that there are just only three independent combinations,
namely
C^{(\prime)}_{T}+C^{(\prime)}_{TP}
,
C^{(\prime)}_{T}+E^{(\prime)}_{\bar{B}T}
and
C^{(\prime)}_{TP}-C^{(\prime)}_{PT,1}
,
that contibute to modes involving flavor exotic states and
P=\pi
or
K
.
These modes are related.
